US bulk fuel oil US Gulf Coast fuel oil bulk prices were mixed during the week
ended Oct. 23, with the very low sulfur (VLSFO) grade tracking underlying swap values and the high sulfur (HSFO) grade moving in line with competitive Platts Market on Close assessment process market activity.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the US Gulf Coast HSFO barge value at $86.45/barrel Oct. 23, down $4.14 from the previous week.
USGC HSFO prices weakened sharply before recovering slightly to end the week, initially on the competitive-offer side, with market activity observed during the MOC.
The first half of the week saw exclusively sell-side activity, with a traded offer from Gunvor reaching as low as a $1/b premium to the Month 1 November USGC HSFO swap.
Buy-side activity could also be observed during the last two MOC sessions of the week, although no bids or offers would test notional value in the Oct. 23 session.
For USGC marine fuel 0.5%S, Platts assessed bulk value up $20.75/metric ton week over week to $773.75/mt Oct. 23, with pricing tracking similar movement in the underlying swaps market amid a lack of competitive market activity for the product during the week.
US fuel oil swaps US Gulf Coast swaps were volatile, not uniformly stronger.
November HSFO fell from $85.90/b Oct. 16 to $79.90/b Oct. 20, then recovered to $82.80/b Oct. 22. November 0.5%-sulfur marine fuel reached $116.50/b Oct. 22, above its $114.70/b level Oct. 16.
HSFO lagged crude. Its November crack against Brent weakened from minus $20.80/b Oct. 16 to minus $25.70/b Oct. 22, even as higher crude prices lifted its outright swap later in the week. The 0.5%-sulfur crack recovered to plus $8/b. Ample Gulf Coast HSFO supply and expensive freight limiting exports were cited for the divergence.
Further attacks or shipping delays near Hormuz and Bab alMandab could lift crude, freight, and fuel oil prices. Conversely, improving flows could ease that risk premium. Asian fuel oil supply remains tight, but high freight may prevent that strength from fully reaching Gulf Coast HSFO cracks.
North America bunkers Tight supply and rising crude and diesel costs pushed some
North American spot retail bunker prices to record levels during the week that ended Oct. 23.
While market volatility had previously kept some buyers and sellers on the sidelines, many who had taken a wait-and-see approach were forced to enter the market.
"The market has been terrible lately," said one bunkers supplier.
Limited availability in most North American ports supported spot bunker fuel prices, while pockets of oversupply in the

southeast pressured marine gasoil prices weaker during the week.
What some traders may have perceived as short-lived surges and volatility in the broader market has proven to be a longerterm trend, and prices have reflected that.
Platts last assessed ex-wharf 3.5%S HSFO in Houston up $40 at $645/mt. That was the highest assessed value since June 8, when it was at $654/mt, according to Platts data. After the market closed Oct. 23, it was heard trading even higher, up to $675/mt.
The same fuel in New Orleans rose $40 to end the week at $845/mt -- its highest value in at least three years.
Ex-wharf Houston 0.5%S VLSFO bunker fuel was last assessed $32 higher at $842/mt Oct. 23. That put it at a five-month high. Ex-wharf New Orleans rose to a near six-month high, gaining $32 to end at $902/mt. After the market closed Oct. 23, it was heard trading even stronger at $925/mt.
The only exception to the strengthening trend was observed at key ports in Charleston and Savannah.

Latin America bunkers Spot 0.5%S VLSFO bunkers pricing moved up in most of the
region's key ports, supported by rallying Singapore prices and a slight decline in supply. Santos, Brazil, saw spot VLSFO rise $39 week over week to $917/mt delivered Oct. 23. In Balboa, Panama, VLSFO was assessed at $939/mt Oct. 23, up $13 from the Oct. 16 assessment.
High diesel prices remained the main driver of spot MGO strength across the region. On Oct. 23, spot MGO in Valparaiso, Chile, was assessed at $1,945/mt delivered Oct. 23, a $43 rise from the Oct. 16 assessment. In contrast, Balboa MGO edged down $1 over the same period, to $1,692/mt ex-wharf.
Americas base oils US base oil spot price decreases were more pronounced
for Group I and most Group II grades amid market pressure to lower prices and indications of product availability, while Group III grades remained steady as maritime disruptions continued mainly in the Strait of Hormuz.
In the Group I segment, Platts assessed domestic SN150 and SN500 spot pricing down 5 cents at $6.45/gallon and $7.45/gal, respectively. In the case of Bright Stock, Platts assessed domestic spot pricing down 15 cents to $9.25/gal, putting domestic at a 5-cent premium over exports, as market indications pointed to a decline in this grade's prices.
For the Group II segment, Platts assessed domestic 100N and 220N spot pricing down 5 cents at $7.15/gal and $7.05/gal,

respectively. Domestic was put at a premium over exports, at $2,200/mt for 100N and $2,140/mt for 220N.
Group III grades remained severely constrained amid US-Iran tensions in the Middle East. Some market participants noted that some customers were reporting severe supply shortages. Despite this, many market participants remained keen to see improvement in the availability of this group.

High freight costs limit fuel oil cargo inflows to Asia

The Asian low sulfur fuel oil market could see further gains in the week to Oct. 30 as high freight rates shuttered arbitrage opportunities, according to local traders.

The high sulfur fuel oil market is expected to trade at elevated levels, despite the recent export tenders for Platts FOB Arab Gulf cargoes, as freight costs and cargo insurance premiums impede shipments out of the Strait of Hormuz.

Platts European Fuel Oil Daily Market Analysis
Arbitrage routes to Singapore, the US shift supply
HSFO markets firm on resupply concerns
European arbitrage opportunities continue to dictate fuel oil fundamentals, while the Platts window sees bidding activity but no trades.
European very low sulfur fuel oil (VLSFO) fundamentals have been largely dictated by arbitrage opportunities to Singapore and the Americas. Market participants, however, reported mixed views on whether these arbitrage routes remained open.

One market participant said VLSFO product in the Mediterranean is moving out of the region "on the USA and Singapore arb."
A second market participant, however, noted that more limited volumes in recent days have acted as a headwind for the local market, contributing to a dip in paper cracks, which recently flipped negative.
According to ship tracking data, about 177,000 metric tons of VLSFO and low sulfur fuel oil (LSFO) products have been loaded in Europe in October so far, destined for Singapore, compared with 682,000 metric tons in September.
European high sulfur fuel oil (HSFO) markets continue to firm as participants note resupply concerns, stemming from a closed arbitrage window from the Americas region.
HSFO supply volumes from the East Mediterranean region, which were significant earlier in the summer, have also lessened, adding to firmer fundamentals.
Softer bunker demand across the region, however, is balancing the supply fundamentals, contributing to a "balanced" market generally.
Within the Platts Market on Close assessment process, Repsol bid for an HSFO cargo on a CIF basis from Barcelona, but did not find a seller.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Tommy Petrou
Feedstocks
European naphtha stays tight as thin supply meets escalating Middle East tensions
Naphtha prices hit multimonth peak at $973.50/mt
Middle East tensions tighten supply availability
Limited activity increases market volatility
The European naphtha price and the prompt backwardation reached multimonth highs on Oct. 23, as limited prompt availability and activity left the market highly sensitive to individual cargo movements, while continued Middle East tensions added uncertainty over refinery supply, crude and freight costs.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the front-month CIF Northwest Europe naphtha swap at $973.50/mt on Oct. 23, the highest price since April 30, when it stood at $1,004.25/mt.
Meanwhile, prompt backwardation also strengthened to a multimonth high. Platts assessed the naphtha CIF NWE M1/M2 timespread at $42.25/mt on Oct. 23, the highest since July 31, when it was assessed at $46/mt.
Escalating tensions in the Middle East have underpinned naphtha outright prices, which are heavily reactive to crude oil price movements.
"With the escalations in the Middle East, it's pushed up a little bit, helped clear some naphtha in Europe and in the Med, and made everything tighter," a trading source said.

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

25

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 26, 2026

Saudi Arabia and Houthi forces exchanged airstrikes over the weekend, which has kept risk premiums high and crude prices elevated.
Significant amounts of refinery capacity remain offline in the Middle East and Russia, reinforcing concerns over product availability despite the postponement of turnarounds in Europe.
Equally, players pointed to a thin market with relatively wide bid-ask spreads, which has increased the volatility in the naphtha market.
"When the market is that thin and there's not really much, every cargo [traded] impacts the price," the trading source added.
European and Mediterranean naphtha has meanwhile been drawn toward Asia amid high east-west spreads, helping clear some surplus availability and further tightening the European balance.
Weaker gas-nap offers relief While softer propane-naphtha spreads have improved
naphtha's competitiveness as a cracker feedstock, petrochemical players continue to operate on a hand-to-mouth basis, only dipping into the buy-side when necessary.
"In Europe, the chems are a bit hand-to-mouth, in Asia as well. I think everyone is running a bit hand-to-mouth, and when they need to buy, they pay whatever price is there because they need to keep running," the trader added.
A softer gasoline complex might help quench some of the demand for naphtha in Europe, with gasoline-naphtha spreads narrowing significantly.
Naphtha blending economics have been eroded substantially as a result, reducing blender's incentive to maximize naphtha's share in the blending pool. This could potentially release more barrels into the petrochemical cracking pool.
However, deteriorating gasoline margins also pose a broader risk to refinery economics. Should weaker product margins ultimately lead refiners to cut runs, any increase in naphtha availability from reduced blending demand could be offset by lower refinery production.
"Gasoline going down also helps the cracking pool and pressures refinery margins, and this may impact the market further," the trader said. "If runs fall, it will be a disaster."
-- Barbara Fernandez-pita
Platts Asia and Middle Eastern Naphtha Daily Market Analysis
India's HMEL seeks up to 55,000 mt naphtha for Nov. 8-25 delivery
Lotte Chemical to reduce Yeosu steam cracker run rate to 70% for Nov
The Asian naphtha market held firm on the back of heightened geopolitical tensions Oct. 26, while high crude prices spurred the naphtha flat price higher.
Platts assessed the benchmark naphtha C+F Japan cargo at $1,030.50/metric ton on Oct. 23 Asian close, rising $22.13/mt

day over day, and $54.50/mt higher week over week, S&P Global data showed.
Geopolitical tensions in the Middle East persist, as renewed Houthi attacks raise risks to Saudi Arabia's oil infrastructure and the export routes the kingdom has increasingly relied on amid disruption to shipping through the Strait of Hormuz.
In the derivatives market, brokers pegged the front-month November-December Mean of Platts Japan swaps time spread at $43/mt in midafternoon Asian trading Oct. 26, up $2.25/mt from the previous close.
In the spot market, India's HMEL has issued a tender seeking either 35,000 mt or 55,000 mt of naphtha cargo, with a minimum 65% paraffin content for Nov. 8-25 loading, via a tender closing Oct. 26, with same-day validity.
In cracker news, South Korea's Lotte Chemical will reduce its naphtha-fed steam cracker's operating rate in Yeosu to about 70% capacity in November, marginally lower than the existing run rate of about 70%-75%, Platts reported earlier. The cracker is able to produce 1.2 million metric tons/year of ethylene, 600,000 mt/ year of propylene and 160,000 mt/year of butadiene.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Zoey Ng

Platts Asian LPG Daily Commentary
CFR North Asia LPG hits 7-month peak
Asian propane-naphtha swaps spread narrows
CFR North Asia LPG prices rose for a third consecutive session to a seven-month high at the start of the Asian trading week on Oct. 26, supported by a trade concluded during the session and a rise in front-month Brent crude futures.
BP was heard to have sold to Trafigura a 23,000-metric-ton propane cargo for delivery in the second half of November on a CFR Japan basis at a premium of $20/mt to the November Far East Index, according to broking sources.
Platts assessed CFR North Asia propane and butane cargoes

at $1,025/mt and $1,115/mt, respectively, Oct. 26, both up $41/mt from the previous session.
China's propane dehydrogenation plant operating rates edged higher in H2 October as units restarted from maintenance, but tight imported LPG availability continued to constrain a broader recovery in run rates.
PDH plant utilization rose to 66.33% as of Oct. 26, from 65.49% a week earlier, according to data from local energy information provider JLC. The rate, however, remained below the September average of 71.39%, suggesting that October utilization would likely remain below the previous month's level.
Operating rates could recover further, with plants totaling 1.35 million mt/year of PDH capacity scheduled to restart following maintenance, potentially providing additional support to China's demand for imported propane, the report showed.
The margin for converting propane into propylene was at 201 yuan/mt as of Oct. 25, compared with an average loss of 36 yuan/ mt in September, according to JLC data.
Platts assessed CFR East China propane at $1028/mt Oct. 26, up $34/mt from the previous session.
Platts assessed CFR South China propane at $1024/mt Oct. 26, up $34/mt day over day.
Platts assessed the CFR North Asia propane-to-C&F Japan naphtha front-month swap spread at minus $14.50/mt Oct. 26, narrowing from minus $23/mt in the previous session.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Jeff Ong

Platts European Propane Daily Commentary
Propane prices rise $18/mt on strong demand
Heating season begins amid low gas storage
Northwest European propane fundamentals tightened Oct. 26, driven by Middle East supply disruptions, high natural gas prices and the start of the heating season.
The Strait of Hormuz remained the focal point, as renewed attacks on ships sharply reduced transits during the month.
Stronger demand from Asia lifted US Gulf Coast netbacks and narrowed the trans-Atlantic arbitrage window for Europe-bound cargoes.
Domestic supply was also constrained, as European natural gas prices climbed steeply month over month, prompting producers to leave more North Sea liquids in the gas stream.
"The propane market is also seeing tightness," a trader said. "However, I believe there is more tightness in the butane market."
This reduced local propane output, while EU gas storage remained well below seasonal norms, leaving the region exposed to a sharp increase in demand in the event of a cold snap.
Seasonally, late October marked the shift into peak retail heating demand, with distributors expected to step up restocking ahead of November.
Petrochemical demand remained a weak element, as elevated LPG values curbed cracker appetite for propane relative to naphtha.
In the CIF large cargo market, no bids, offers, or trades were heard in the Platts Market on Close assessment process. In the absence of competitive indications, the market was assessed on trader sentiment.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the CIF large cargo market in outright terms at $850/metric ton, up $18/mt day over day, at a $30/mt premium over the propane CIF November swap, down $2/mt from premium levels.
In the propane coaster market, no bids, offers or trades were heard in the MOC. In the absence of competitive indications, the market was assessed on trader sentiment.

Platts assessed the propane coaster market at $910/mt, up $18/mt day over day, at a $60/mt premium to large cargoes.
In the propane barge market, no bids, offers, or trades were heard in the MOC. In the absence of competitive indications, the market was assessed on trader sentiment.
Platts assessed the FOB Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp barges stable day over day at plus $65/mt versus the CIF large propane market, while FCA ARA market premiums to CIF large were assessed at plus $80/mt.
-- Baran Serdaroglu
Platts European Butane Daily Commentary
Global butane shortage drives price gains
Rhine water levels affect barge transport
Weak petrochemical demand limits consumption
Butane remained the firmer end of the European LPG barrel Oct. 26, as a global shortage continued to outweigh muted petrochemical demand.
Tightness was evident across regions, with US Gulf Coast butane trading at a wide premium to propane through October, largely closing the arbitrage for US butane US cargoes into Europe outside of term contracts.
In Asia, physical butane cargoes widened their premium over propane amid concerns over supply availability.
Seasonal factors also underpinned demand, as the switch to winter-grade gasoline typically increased butane blending requirements during the fourth quarter, keeping refiners and blenders active in the market.
Barge availability in the Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp hub also remained sensitive to Rhine water levels as levels at Kaub rose to 56 centimeters at 1 pm local time Oct. 26, from 31 cm a day earlier, and were forecast to reach 76 cm Oct. 28, according to data and forecasts published on Germany's official waterways information service ELWIS.
Petrochemical demand remained the main offset to the tighter fundamentals; with butane priced above propane and naphtha, crackers had limited incentive to increase butane consumption.
Asian flexible crackers were expected to reduce LPG intake again in November, extending the downtrend seen since the summer, while European demand from crackers similarly remained limited.
In the butane CIF NWE large cargo market, no bids, offers, or trades were heard in the Platts Market on Close assessment process. In the absence of competitive indications, the market was assessed on trader sentiment.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the CIF large cargo market in outright terms at $1,099.75/mt, up $8.75/mt day over day and stable as a percentage to Platts CIF NWE naphtha at 112%.
In the absence of indications in the Platts Market on Close assessment process, the butane coaster market was assessed on trader sentiment.

Platts Western Mediterranean LPG Daily Commentary
Butane supply tightens on refinery retention
FOB West Med butane rises $11/mt to $1,257
Import options remain limited amid competition
West Mediterranean butane availability remained constrained on Oct. 26 as refinery retention for winter gasoline blending limited supplies reaching the merchant market, while restricted import options offered little relief.
A trader highlighted a "seasonal increase in demand," as winter gasoline specifications continued to underpin butane requirements within refinery blending pools.
Refiners prioritized internal requirements, reducing volumes available for spot sales and making prompt cargoes difficult to source.
The trader also pointed to "difficulty sourcing available volumes."
Import availability remained constrained. Reduced Middle Eastern exports tightened the wider butane balance, while competition from Asian buyers for US LPG limited opportunities for replenishment from the Atlantic Basin.
Higher freight costs and limited availability of smaller vessels added to the difficulty of securing workable deliveries into the Mediterranean.
Petrochemical demand provided little additional support, with butane remaining unattractive relative to alternative feedstocks. As a result, the market remained primarily driven by seasonal gasoline blending requirements and restricted merchant supply.
The combination of refinery retention, limited import relief and logistical constraints maintained a firm underlying balance, while weaker petrochemical interest and selective spot buying tempered broader demand.
Platts assessed the West Mediterranean FOB butane market at $1,257/metric ton, up $11/mt day over day and stable as a percentage versus naphtha at 128%, or at a $39/mt discount to FOB Northwest Europe coasters.
Platts assessed coaster freight from Lavera to Mohammedia at $66/mt, steady day over day, based on market information received throughout the day.
The CIF Mediterranean butane large cargo market was

assessed at $1,129.75/mt, or 115% of naphtha. Platts assessed the West Mediterranean FOB propane market
at $972.50/mt, up $18/mt day over day. The CIF Med propane large cargo market was assessed at
$867.75/mt, or at a $17.75/mt premium to CIF Northwest Europe large cargoes.
In Morocco, Platts calculated the CIF very large cargo freight forward at $994.25/mt for delivery 45-60 days forward, up $6.75/mt.

Tankers
Saudi shipper Bahri expands tanker fleet with $451M VLCC order
Three 320,000 DWT vessels to be delivered by 2029-30
Fleet reaches 107 vessels end of June
Deal brings Bahri's newbuild orders to nine vessels
The National Shipping Co. of Saudi Arabia has signed a $451 million deal with domestic shipbuilder International Maritime Industries Co. to construct and deliver three very large crude carriers, Bahri said in a statement Oct. 25 to the Saudi stock exchange.
The order, signed on Oct. 22, will involve the manufacture of three VLCCs with a capacity of 320,000 deadweight tons each, with delivery to Bahri scheduled in batches between 2029 and 2030, it said in the statement.
Bahri's fleet, which was fully commercially deployed over the second quarter despite the Middle East conflict, stood at "a record" 107 vessels at the end of June 2026, including 49 oil carriers as of the end of the second quarter, the company said July 29 in a quarterly earnings release.
The three vessels will bring the total number of newbuild ships being constructed in Saudi Arabia for Bahri to nine, comprising six dry bulk carriers and three VLCCs, Bahri said.
In October 2025, Bahri signed a deal to purchase six geared Ultramax dry bulk vessels from IMI to be constructed in the shipbuilder's Ras al-Khair shipyard in Saudi Arabia and see delivery between 2028 and 2029, Platts reported Oct. 2, 2025.
Bahri will fund its latest contract through a combination of internal cash resources and banking facilities, according to the statement, while the contract term will end upon the delivery of the last vessel to the company.
The financial impact of the contract will be reflected on Bahri's financial statements after the vessels are received and commercial operations begin, the company said.
Bahri holds a 19.9% equity stake in IMI, a subsidiary of Saudi Arabian Oil Co., while Bahri CEO Ali al-Subaey also serves on IMI's board of directors, according to the company's statement.

VLCC transits climb at Hormuz; steady at Bab al-Mandab for second day
Hormuz sees six VLCCs, most since Oct 17
Bab al-Mandab records one VLCC for two days
US marks 1.5 billion barrels through Hormuz
The Strait of Hormuz had six VLCC transits on Oct. 25, the most since Oct. 17, while the Bab al-Mandab Strait recorded one VLCC on both Oct. 24 and Oct. 25, S&P Global Commodities at Sea said in an Oct. 26 report.
Hormuz traffic in total was steady at 17 ships on Oct. 24 and Oct. 25, while Bab al-Mandab transits jumped to 42 ships from 27 ships over the same period, according to the CAS report. The VLCC total for Hormuz on Oct. 25 was up from five on Oct. 24, CAS data showed.
The outbound VLCC from the Persian Gulf was Monaco Prosperity, while inbound VLCCs were Bangladesh Prosperity, Ennie, Thailand Prosperity, West Loyalty and Bulgaria Prosperity, CAS said. The VLCCs that transited Bab al-Mandab were Della, heading northbound, on Oct. 25 and Karina, also moving northbound, on Oct. 24, according to CAS.
US forces on Oct. 25 marked the export of at least 1.5 billion barrels of crude oil since the summer through Hormuz, with US Central Command commander Admiral Brad Cooper flying over the chokepoint in a US Army CH-47 Chinook, Central Command said on X. It noted that more than 3,000 commercial ships had successfully transited Hormuz with support from CENTCOM forces over the last six months.
"Today, primary transit routes in the Strait of Hormuz remain free and clear of Iranian sea mines thanks to incredible efforts by our people," Cooper said. "We remain intensely focused on keeping commercial traffic flowing as we work with our teammates in the US interagency, Gulf Cooperation Council, and shipping industry."
The comment came despite Iran launching its heaviest wave of attacks on merchant ships around Hormuz and in the wider region this month, with the International Maritime Organization recording 29 attacks during Oct. 1-22, exceeding all monthly tallies since March.
On Oct. 26, the UK Maritime Trade Operations Centre reported a tanker exiting Hormuz was disabled after being struck by an unknown projectile Oct. 23. No crew casualties or oil spills have been reported.
Of the 16 VLCC crossings identified over Oct. 23-25, 10 were assessed as inbound and six outbound, CAS said. Inbound VLCC movements included five of South Korea's Sinokor-operated ships, and the Saudi-linked Majra and Qasba. The VLCC Ennie moved into the Persian Gulf after last loading a cargo of Basrah Heavy crude in early October, and China-owned VLCC Indigo Ray also moved inbound, CAS said.
Outbound transits included Sinokor-operated Romania Prosperity, UAE-linked Das and Al Faya, and Saudi-linked Kahla and Emerald Shine, CAS said.

LPG tankers Over Oct. 23-25, five LPG tankers were observed crossing
the Strait of Hormuz, including one outbound and four inbound transits, CAS said.
The outbound ship was the Marshall Islands-flagged Very Large Gas Carrier Sheng Hai Long, which had been in the Persian Gulf since Aug. 21, CAS said. During its stay, the ship loaded LPG at Ras Laffan, Qatar, while operating without AIS visibility, then exited the strait in a dark transit before reappearing in the Arabian Sea on Oct. 24, CAS said.
On Oct. 24, two LPG tankers entered the Gulf. One was UAElinked Sweihan, which previously transferred cargo at Vadinar, India, and was last seen heading for Ruwais, UAE, for its next loading, CAS said. On Oct. 25, UAE-linked LPG tanker Delma was last seen berthed at Ras Laffan for loading, CAS said.
Activity across the US naval blockade line came to 57 transits on Oct. 25, up from 51 on Oct. 24, according to CAS.
-- Claudia Carpenter, Max Lin
Alberta backs bill to remove crude tanker ban in Canadian Pacific
Bill scheduled for debate on Oct. 28 Sees options for crude-by-rail exports
Alberta has extended its support for a private bill in the Canadian Parliament to repeal the federal Oil Tanker Moratorium Act, which is scheduled for debate on Oct. 28, a provincial government statement said Oct. 26.
The provincial government supports private member's Bill C-264, and repealing the ban would open the door to crude-byrail exports through Prince Rupert in coastal British Columbia, giving the province another route to reach customers around the world, Premier Danielle Smith said in a statement.
"Our oil to the United States is selling at a significant discount and at a disadvantage to the entire country [Canada]," Smith said. "The only effective way to close that gap is to increase exports off our western coast and get more customers competing for our energy. For decades, limited access to tidewater has left Alberta heavily dependent on a single customer, putting our oil at a competitive disadvantage and reducing the value of our resources."

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 26, 2026

In March, Conservative Member of Parliament David McKenzie from the Calgary Signal Hill riding in Alberta first tabled the bill for discussion in parliament.
The Conservative Party of Canada is the official opposition in parliament (House of Commons) with 137 members, with a total of 342 seats.
"The tanker ban remains a stumbling block to accessing Asia, one of the fastest-growing markets in the world," Alberta's Energy and Minerals Minister Brian Jean said in a statement. "Sailing from Prince Rupert would provide the shortest energy shipping route to Asia. This means lower shipping costs, less fuel used and fewer emissions created. Producers have already expressed interest in using this route for crude oil exports."
In June 2019, the federal government enacted the Oil Moratorium Act, which banned the movement of tankers laden with over 12,500 metric tons (91,625 barrels) of crude oil in the area from the Canada/US border in the north, down to the point on British Columbia's mainland across from the northern tip of Vancouver Island. It also included Haida Gwaii and the waters of Dixon Entrance, Hecate Strait and Queen Charlotte Sound.
The oil tanker ban does not apply to natural gas or NGL or any other commodity carried by tankers, Jean said, adding that Canada has one of the best marine safety and environmental records in the world.
"We safely manage tanker navigation in busy and difficult waters on both the west and east coasts without incident. We can also do so with oil tankers carrying Alberta oil," Jean said.
-- Ashok Dutta
Platts East of Suez Clean Tanker Daily Commentary
Chartering STS ships delayed
Cross-harbor Singapore freight falls below $1 million
MRs garner higher earnings in Singapore vs Oman
Medium Range, or MRs, were mostly lower Oct. 26 amid ample tanker supply.
South Korea remains the primary source of activity in Northeast Asia, with China's export volumes easing due to restrictions, an MR broker said.
Charterers are expected to take advantage of the small decline in freight by covering their requirements for loading early next month. Pertamina is seeking two MRs: one for Nov. 2 loading on the Singapore-Tuban route, with an option to discharge in Teluk Kabung, and another for Nov. 8 loading on the SingaporeBau Bau route, sources said. Petrolimex is seeking an MR for Nov. 4 loading on the Thailand-Ho Chi Minh route, sources added.
Returns are higher for loading cargoes in Southeast Asia for delivery Down Under than for Oman or West Coast India loading for East and South Africa delivery, said a source at a clean-oil tanker owner. Daily scrubber-fitted MR earnings on the benchmark Singapore-Australia route are close to $67,000$77,000 on a round-voyage basis, assuming HSFO bunker prices of around $875/metric ton, according to broker estimates. Corresponding daily earnings on the West Coast India/Oman-to-

East Africa route are around $57,000-$58,000, assuming HSFO bunker prices of around $730/mt, they said. For older MRs, these earnings are even lower, at around $54,000, they added.
Though Typhoon Champi is projected to reach coastlines soon, the logistical impact on shipments is expected to remain limited due to its weak intensity, a second MR broker said. The potential impact of the approaching Typhoon No. 31 on upcoming activities remains uncertain, a third broker said.
Among outstanding cargoes, Ampol is seeking an MR for Nov. 4-6 loading on the Korea-Australia route, sources said.
Southeast Asia MRs inched lower on softening sentiment for longer voyages. With slowing chartering activity and MRs ballasting toward Southeast Asia, freight is poised to soften, a clean tankers' broker said.
The Silver Monika was placed on subjects by Pertamina for Oct. 28 loading on the Singapore-Tanjung Uban route, and rate details were not known, sources said. Cross-harbor MR voyages in Singapore are expected to be below the psychological mark of $1 million, brokers said.
Long Range, or LRs, were unchanged due to slow market activity, market participants said. Due to the Hormuz War, charterers are uncertain about the fate of the "shuttle tankers" bringing out cargoes through the risky waters of the Persian Gulf, one of the brokers said. The tankers may get stuck in the narrow waterway, or their transit can get inordinately delayed due to the war, which in turn will hurt the itinerary of the shipto-ship, or STS, transfer in Oman or West Coast India, the broker said. Therefore, charterers are now hiring LRs for STS transfers outside the Persian Gulf at much shorter, or prompt, notice after they are convinced about the transit of the shuttle tankers, he said. According to market participants, this can make the overall supply of tankers look bigger than it actually is.
An LR broker said that next-done transactions on LR1s could be tracking close to recent Africa-bound voyages, citing the recent Gem No. 3 taken by Trafigura at w500.
Among outstanding cargoes, Keumeur is seeking a tanker for Oct. 27 naphtha loading on the Fujairah-East route, though it was heard downsizing to an MR instead, sources said. Shell is seeking an LR1 for end-October loading on the Sohar-West route via the Suez Canal, sources added.
In recent fixtures, the LR1 Torm Eva was placed on subjects at w535 for Oct. 28 loading on the STS Oman-Japan route, and the Amazon Fortitude was replaced by the Hafnia Excellence at w550 by OQ after being released, sources said.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Sameer Mohindru, Ellie Lin

News
Energy markets unlikely to return to prewar conditions in 2027: IEA's Birol
IEA warns trust becomes scarcest resource
Nuclear capacity construction hits 40-year high
ASEAN nations accelerate electricity shift
Global energy markets are unlikely to return to prewar conditions in 2027, International Energy Agency Executive Director Fatih Birol said during an industry event on Oct. 26, while urging governments and businesses to prioritize energy security as Southeast Asia faces heightened exposure to supply disruptions.
"I am afraid I don't see any credible pathway that the markets will go next year, where we were before this war has started," Birol said during the Singapore International Energy Week, or SIEW, 2026.
"So therefore, we all need to get prepared, taking energy security as a number one priority in our government, business world, but without forgetting what is the next chapter for the countries and for companies that are all here today is," Birol continued.
"If you ask me in today's energy world, everything included, what is the most scarce commodity? Is it oil? No. Is it natural gas? No. The most scarce commodity today in the energy world is trust," he said.
People are looking for trusted partners, and it goes both ways for importers and for exporters, Birol said.
"If an exporting country uses its energy as a political leverage, I can tell you they are facing a reputation risk, which may end up in their businesses," he said.
Birol said countries such as Saudi Arabia, the UAE and other Middle Eastern countries responded swiftly to supply disruptions

by rerouting energy shipments and building or expanding pipelines to bypass the Strait of Hormuz.
Meanwhile, many ASEAN countries started reviewing energy strategies, with sources such as hydropower, geothermal energy, and natural gas, and even mulling over nuclear power for electricity generation.
"Some two years ago, I said the world is entering the age of electricity [electrification]. Now, I can assure you, this energy crisis will even accelerate our entering the age of electricity," Birol said.
"We will still use oil. We will still use gas. But the electrification rate is very strong, driven by traditional factors, data centers, electric cars, and a skyrocketing number of air conditioners," prompting countries to look at various sources of electricity generation, Birol said.
"Nuclear power is coming back," not only with traditional plants but also with advanced technologies, as countries seek more reliable and diversified electricity sources amid persistent geopolitical risks, Birol said.
According to IEA estimates, around 90 gigawatts of nuclear power capacity is currently under construction globally -- the highest level of nuclear construction in four decades, Birol said.
Birol also said that the ASEAN power grid was a "no-brainer" for any country in this region to enhance energy security while also achieving the goal of carbon reduction.
He noted that the IEA is working with countries in the region on a range of energy initiatives -- from building oil stocks together to other energy security steps.
Birol stressed the need for energy diversification to mitigate supply disruptions and complimented Singapore on making great strides with its energy policy.
In 2024, the IEA opened its first-ever office outside its Paris headquarters in Singapore to deepen its engagement in the region.
-- Surabhi Sahu, Mia Pei
Algeria to send troops to Niger to protect energy facilities
Algeria's first foreign deployment since 1973
Sonatrach is drilling in Niger's Kafra oil field
Nations are Trans-Saharan gas pipeline partners
Algeria is set to deploy military units to neighboring Niger to protect energy infrastructure in the unstable Sahel nation, where Algeria's national oil company is drilling for crude, according to a statement from the presidency.

The foreign deployment - Algeria's first since 1973 - comes after Niger's defense minister visited Algiers to seek help in the West African country's fight against terrorist groups, including those linked to al-Qaeda and the Islamic State.
It also comes after Algeria sent fighter jets to Niger in August to help see off an attempted coup against Gen. Abdourahamane Tchiani, who took power in Niger in 2023 in a coup of his own that ousted elected president Mohamed Bazoum.
The Algerian presidency did not provide any details about the likely length or scope of the deployment or the number of troops that could be involved.
State-run Sonatrach - which has been expanding its African footprint in the past year - is currently undertaking hydrocarbon exploration in Niger's Kafra oil field.

The two countries are also partners in the Trans Saharan Gas Pipeline, an ambitious project designed to move gas from Nigeria to Algeria and onto Europe via Niger. The project was thrown into jeopardy after the 2023 Niger coup, which strained relations between Niamey and Abuja.
Nevertheless, Algerian officials remain optimistic, with the country starting construction on its leg of the pipe in June, according to its hydrocarbon ministry.
Niger is reliant on crude exports and imported refined products. It exports around 110,000 barrels/day of its heavy sweet Meleck crude through the Niger-Benin pipeline and onto international markets.

Water levels on Rhine rise, but relief forecast to be short-lived
Rhine water levels reach 56 cm at Kaub
Switzerland releases emergency diesel stocks
Freight costs remain elevated at 255/mt
Water levels on the Rhine, a critical European inland waterway for freight, rose above 50 centimeters Oct. 26 and are forecast to hover around 70 cm in the next few days, but the rebound in flows is expected to provide only short-term relief to fuel supply chains across the region, according to market participants.
Water levels at Kaub, the shallowest part of the Middle Rhine, stood at 56 cm as of late afternoon Oct. 26 and are forecast to exceed 70 cm by the end of the day, then expected to hold around that level for the following three days, according to data from the German water authority WSV.
The Rhine water levels exceeded 50 cm on Oct. 26 for the first time since Sept. 4, with levels mostly below 10 cm from Sept. 24 to Oct. 10 and mostly below 20 cm from Oct. 11 to Oct. 22.
The recent rise in Rhine water levels has generated renewed market activity, but the improvement is expected to be short-

lived, according to a European-based market participant who asked for anonymity because the participant was not authorized to speak publicly. The source said extremely low water levels in recent weeks had forced refiners to rely more heavily on trucks, rail, pipelines, and some coaster movements to transport products, although overall product movements remained slow.
A second market participant similarly said the latest rise is unlikely to provide meaningful relief, as the window for barges to pass the Maxau area is expected to be too short to materially ease logistical constraints. Conditions are particularly tight in Switzerland, according to the source, where product inventories are extremely low and market participants are focused on maintaining supplies to retail stations.
The Swiss government authorized the release of diesel inventories Oct. 23 to address shortages connected to pipeline delivery delays due to heavy rainfall in southern France. Switzerland also approved the release of jet fuel stocks earlier this month.
"Switzerland's logistical supply has been severely affected for months by the persistent low water levels on the Rhine. If a problem arises with one of the other modes of transport, this can quickly lead to a bottleneck," the Swiss Federal Office for National Economic Supply said.
Germany is comparatively less affected, according to the market participant, because of its larger refining base and less severe isolation from Rhine supply routes, although low water levels continue to restrict river transportation.
That said, rail options in Germany remain limited due to the ongoing closure for refurbishment of the right-bank Middle Rhine Line, a key German freight route. The line is expected to stay closed until Dec. 12.
"The situation for refiners along the Rhine remains broadly unchanged in October, with the month-to-date average water level at Kaub at around 14 cm. Crude supply remains relatively stable, but the main logistical challenge is clearing refined products amid higher freight costs due to highly limited options," said Sammir Lesage, analyst at S&P Global Energy CERA.
"Those additional costs are likely to be passed through the supply chain, with consumers ultimately bearing much of the burden", Lesage added.

UK floats fast-track approvals for 'urgent' oil, gas tiebacks
Regulator NSTA urged to weigh applications
Tiebacks extend existing field lifespans
Follows Jackdaw sign-off, Rosebank still due
The UK government could grant licenses to a small number of "urgent" tieback projects - designed to extend lifespans of existing oil and gas fields - before new tieback legislation is introduced, according to an intergovernmental letter released Oct. 26, in the latest sign of a shifting energy approach from Andy Burnham's government.
In a letter to Stuart Payne, chief executive of the upstream regulator the North Sea Transition Authority, Michael Shanks, the minister of state for energy, reiterated plans to introduce Transitional Energy Certificates allowing companies to go after known hydrocarbon deposits close to existing fields, while maintaining its ban on new exploration.
"These reforms will be brought forward through legislation when parliamentary time allows," Shanks wrote in the letter dated Oct. 21 and posted Oct. 26 on the NSTA's website. "In the meantime, we recognize that a small number of projects may face near-term investment and operational timelines that cannot await the passage of the legislation."
He added: "I am therefore asking the NSTA to consider whether there are any such projects that may warrant an earlier licensing decision so that the NSTA can take action to invite, assess and determine applications for them."

The minister noted that the move should not "be regarded as a new plan or program of work or wider reopening of licenses." Instead it will apply in "urgent cases," he said, adding that the number of projects meeting the criteria will likely "be very limited."
The NSTA declined to comment on which fields or projects could see rapid pre-legislation decisions.
The review of "urgent cases" was floated by the Department of Energy Security and Net Zero on the approval of Adura's contentious Jackdaw gas field last week. "This will ensure the sector can support existing oil and gas fields, while still preventing exploration in new unlicensed areas," the department said.
Alongside the Jackdaw approval, urgent tieback permits provided before the legislation is introduced could herald a shift in approach from Burnham's government, amid rising energy security concerns in the UK. Industry insiders say modern horizontal drilling techniques allow for hydrocarbon exploitation at considerable distances from existing fields.
The new prime minister has signaled he would take a more pragmatic approach than his predecessor Keir Starmer, who angered industry players by extending and increasing the unpopular Energy Profts Levy, which has raised the headline tax rate to 78% in the North Sea.
The EPL and bans on new drilling have been blamed for a wave of IOC exits and consolidation deals on the UK Continental Shelf, as well as a drop in national oil and gas production. It is set to be replaced with a less stringent Oil and Gas Price Mechanism in or before 2030, according to officials.
New energy secretary Miatta Fahnbulleh is yet to make a decision on the 70,000 b/d Rosebank oil and gas field, which like Jackdaw has been subject to legal challenges.
Failure to approve it could make the UK oil sector uninvestable, according to Rosebank stakeholder Equinor.
UK crude output has fallen by about two-thirds since 2009 to roughly 600,000 b/d, according to official figures. The country's Brent and Forties crude grades underpin the Platts Dated Brent benchmark, last assessed at $126.69/b on Oct. 23. Platts is part of S&P Global Energy.
-- Charlie Mitchell, Matt Hoisch
Portugal's Galp lifts refining margin outlook amid full availability
Galp raises 2026 refining margin to $25/b
Sines refinery fully available until 2027
Brazilian output reaches 129,000 boe/d

Portugal's Galp Energia SGPS SA said Oct. 26 it has increased its full-year 2026 refining margin guidance to $25/barrel following a strong third quarter.
The level, based principally on benchmark Rotterdam hydrocracking and cracking margins, is up from a previous forecast of $13/b and follows the realized margin for the first three quarters more than tripling year on year to $22.10/b, company data shows.

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

36

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 26, 2026

In Q3 the margin was $34.00/b as middle distillate cracks surged, and Galp said its system operated at close to full rates to capture "elevated" international products cracks, particularly for diesel and jet.
The company said in July it expected throughput for the full year in a range between 80 million barrels and 85 million barrels (about 219,000-233,000 b/d) following robust operations in Q2, with no update provided since.
The 226,000 b/d Sines refinery started a hydrocracker maintenance Sept. 24 that is expected to end around Nov. 1, but "no major turnaround [is] planned to 2027," co-CEO Joao Diogo Marques da Silva told analysts on a conference call.
Galp said it has "been actively mitigating global market disruptions, having already secured all Portugal's jet import requirements for the remainder of the year."
The margin currently sits at around $20/b, Marques da Silva said, noting that there are "sustainable fundamentals to assume double-digit margin to 2027, at least in the first quarter."
The company is also in the "final ramp up of the major investments" that it is making in Sines, Marques da Silva said.
Galp plans to bring online two low-carbon projects at the industrial complex, a 270,000 metric ton/year advanced biofuels unit for HVO/SAF production and a 100 MW electrolyzer plant for the production of green hydrogen.
"We are in the pre-commissioning phase right now," Marques da Silva said, without adding details.
Upstream update In the upstream business, Galp's "execution remains firmly
on track," co-CEO Maria Joao Carioca said. Galp has forecast reaching around 130,000 b/d of oil equivalent from its Brazilian upstream operations and hit an average 129,000 boe/d working interest production in the first nine months of 2026.
Ramp-up of the Equinor-operated Bacalhau project in the Santos basin is "progressing as planned, with production already reaching peak levels on several days [..] with four producer wells connected," Carioca said.
The field has a production target of 220,000 boe/d by yearend, with 40,000 boe/d net to Galp. Equinor did not respond to a request for comment from Platts, part of S&P Global Energy.
Galp has added to its Brazil portfolio through the award of the Rodocrosita block, neighboring Bacalhau, which should secure additional long-term optionality and the potential to unlock future synergies.
Galp declined to give upstream guidance for 2027, but noted that Bacalhau should produce at plateau, while other Brazilian assets are seeing depletion rates of less than 5%.
In Namibia, preparation works for the next exploration and appraisal campaign at Mopane continue with the drilling of the next well imminent, Galp said, while a final investment decision for the Venus field could be taken "soon," according to Carioca.
The company's midstream operations had seen "disrupted trading dynamics, in particular elevated freight costs and softer crude premiums amid weaker Asian demand," the company noted.

Its oil realization indicator, estimated by applying the average discount or premium to Brent of all negotiated cargoes, net of logistics costs, to the average Brent price for the same period, was $87.30/b in Q3, narrowing from $102.90/b in Q2.
Freight costs have risen from around $3-$4/b to over $20/b in October, which has led the company to consider "options" in terms of extending mid-term charters, and not rely so much on the spot market, Carioca said.
-- Gianluca Baratti
INDIA DATA: Indian refineries processed 5.62 mil b/d in Sep: Oil Min
Crude processed in Sep up 9.1% on year, up 2.7% on month
Sep crude imports 10.2% up on year, up 20% on month
Sep oil demand 2% up on year, 6.4% up on month
Indian refineries processed 5.62 million b/d in September, the oil ministry's provisional estimates showed, reflecting 9.1% higher processing from a year ago as imports by the world's third-largest buyer rose despite supply concerns due to the Middle East conflict.
The crude processed in September was 6.6% higher than the month's target of 5.27 million b/d, it said.
The processed volume in September was 2.7% higher than 5.47 million b/d in August, the ministry said.
In the April-September period or H1, Indian refiners processed 5.45 million b/d, up 1.5% from a year ago, it said, reflecting improved runs during the first half of the fiscal year despite maintenance works carried out by private refiners like Nayara and Reliance during the first quarter.
India processed 1.3% year-over-year higher crude volume at 5.46 million b/d in 2025-26 (April-March) or FY26, according to the ministry's provisional data, aided by cheaper Russian crude imports.
India, the world's fourth-largest refiner, has 5.35 million b/d installed crude processing capacity, according to the oil ministry.
India, also the world's third-largest crude consumer and importer, sources 88% of its domestic crude demand through imports, it said.
Imports, domestic demand In September, Indian refiners imported 5.4 million b/d,
according to the oil ministry's data, up 10.2% year over year and up 20% from August.
In the April-September period or H1, Indian refiners imported 4.9 million b/d, the same volume as a year ago, it said.
Indian refineries imported 4.9 million b/d in FY26, the same as a year ago, the latest provisional data showed.
In September, India's oil product demand stood at 5 million b/d, up 2% year over year, the oil ministry said.
In H1, India's oil demand stood at 4.9 million b/d, down 3.9% from a year ago, it said.
India's oil product demand was 5.2 million b/d in FY26, up 2% from a year ago, the ministry said.
-- Ratnajyoti Dutta

Equinor, Aker BP collaboration eyes higher Norway oil and gas output
Companies to collaborate on tight reservoirs
NOD sees 2.8 billion boe recoverable potential in such reservoirs
Equinor aims to sustain NCS output into 2035
Norway's Equinor and Aker BP are beginning a strategic collaboration around tight reservoirs aimed at boosting oil and natural gas development and recovery on the Norwegian Continental Shelf, both said in statements on Oct. 26.
The companies will collaborate on technology development, standardization and knowledge sharing to support work on prospects, discoveries and projects in tight reservoirs across the NCS where oil and gas flow particularly slowly though rock, they said.
The Norwegian Offshore Directorate has estimated some 2.8 billion barrels of oil equivalent in recoverable resource potential from such mapped reservoirs, which include many of the roughly 90 discoveries that have yet to get a development decision, both said.
"The Norwegian Offshore Directorate has therefore encouraged companies to work together," Aker BP said in its statement.
"Scale is the key," Aker BP CEO Karl Johnny Hersvik said. "By considering opportunities across both portfolios, we can create the basis for increased activity, faster learning and lower costs. Together, we can develop solutions more quickly and deploy them at a larger scale. We are starting this work immediately."
Equinor's Executive Vice President for Exploration & Production in Norway, Kjetil Hove, said: "By viewing our portfolios in context and developing common solutions, we can reduce costs and develop and qualify technology faster."
NCS outlook State-owned Equinor aims to sustain production of about
1.3 million barrels of oil equivalent/day on the NCS into 2035. It has estimated that about 70% of this output will come from new wells and developments.
Gas output on the NCS is expected to hold roughly steady over the coming years, thanks to new developments and exploration, before falling toward the end of the decade, the Norwegian Offshore Directorate said in its latest annual report on the NCS in January.
Gas output across the NCS is projected to fall to 107.1 Bcm by 2030, from some 119.8 Bcm in 2025, according to the NOD.
Norway is a key gas supplier for Europe. Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the month-ahead Dutch TTF gas price at 92.78/megawatt-hour on Oct. 23. The index is at its highest point since December 2022.
-- Matt Hoisch

Brazil's oil, gas sector to maintain growth after Lula's victory
Fourth non-consecutive term
Oil output record level in August
Opening up domestic gas market
Brazil's oil and natural gas industry will stay on pace for higher production and increased exploration activity to rebuild reserves after President Luiz Inacio Lula da Silva was reelected for a fourth term, but Latin America's largest economy also will seek to leverage renewable energy sources to attract investments in data centers and AI.
Brazil's energy policies weren't much of a focus during the election, except for some increased chatter surrounding the potential privatization of state-led oil company Petrobras, government and industry officials told Platts, part of S&P Global Energy. Bolsonaro had dismissed talk that his administration would sell off stakes in state-controlled entities as "fake news."
Lula, meanwhile, sought investors to build data centers powered by renewable energy sources and provide the government with 10% capacity to develop local technology and AI initiatives, officials noted. Lula also wants to develop and refine Brazil's deposits of rare earth minerals needed for the global energy transition.
Lula and his Workers' Party, or PT, have favored a state-led development model that included national champions such as Petrobras as key investors in strategic areas such as refining and fertilizers. Lula also has suggested that Petrobras needs to return to the retail distribution market after its non-compete clause against the former distribution unit Vibra ends in mid-2029.
Despite that interventionist approach, the PT's management of the oil and gas sector has generated relatively little controversy and delivered favorable results, helping sustain investment and production growth, said Marco Oviedo, senior economist and strategist at XP Inc, in Sao Paulo.
"It's one of the sectors that has shown strong momentum in recent years," Oviedo said. "The potential remains, and it will continue to be a sector that generates growth."
Petrobras' controlling stake in Vibra was sold in two stages, completed in July 2019 and July 2021 as part of privatization efforts conducted under the Bolsonaro administration. Lula ended Petrobras' asset sales program when he was inaugurated for a third non-consecutive term in January 2023.
Lula will also likely maintain existing subsidies on diesel sales and imports, officials said. The subsidies have been funded by cuts to fuel taxes and revenues, including a 12% tax on crude oil exports that is also likely to stay in the near term.
Plans to expand Brazil's domestic refining capacity will also likely be maintained under the ongoing Lula administration, with Petrobras CEO Magda Chambriard confirming in September that the company wants to meet 100% of the country's diesel demand under the upcoming 2027-2031 investment plan. The plan is expected to be released by the end of November.

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

38

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 26, 2026

Lula won 51.79% of the vote in a second-round runoff against challenger Flavio Bolsonaro, who received 48.21%. The final tally represented a political turnaround for Lula, who trailed Bolsonaro in the first round held Oct. 4 at 45.16%. Bolsonaro, the son of former president Jair Bolsonaro, led the first round at 47.03%.
The election outcome caught financial markets by surprise, with investors having largely anticipated a victory by Bolsonaro, according to Oviedo.
The initial negative market reaction reflected that unexpected result, but investors would eventually adjust their expectations, Oviedo said.
Record production, exploration success Lula's reelection also came amid a string of production
records from subsalt production sharing fields discovered during his first two terms. Chambriard has accelerated the installation of floating production units, with five pumping first oil from the Buzios and Mero fields since October 2024.
Brazil's oil output set a third-consecutive monthly production record in August at 4.615 million b/d, topping the previous monthly record of 4.498 million b/d set in July, the National Petroleum Agency, or ANP, said Oct. 1. Subsalt fields accounted for 82.6% of the country's total oil and gas output at 3.842 million b/d and 177.6 million cubic meters/d, or 4.959 million boe/d, from 203 wells.
Brazil's ability to attract international oil companies reflects not only its substantial prospective resources but also the governance framework established through reforms at Petrobras, Oviedo said.
That framework has helped make Petrobras a reliable partner for international investors, distinguishing Brazil from other regional producers such as Mexico's Pemex, where government policy exerts much more direct influence over corporate decisions, he said.
Petrobras also officially opened Brazil's equatorial margin, widely considered one of the country's last two remaining exploration frontiers. Petrobras made two separate oil discoveries in the Morpho well drilled in the Foz do Amazonas Basin. That was the first major exploration well in the region in more than 15 years.
While Petrobras received an amended license to drill three additional wells, the company still remains waiting on environmental licenses to tap other exploration wells in a region that geologists believe holds similar oil and natural gas deposits as those found in Guyana, Ghana and Suriname. The Lula administration has backed exploration in the region in an effort to rebuild Brazil's oil and gas reserves as the subsalt region matures.
Brazil's subsalt oil and gas production is expected to peak in the early to mid-2030s, with additional exploration needed to mitigate expected future declines. The expected downturn has raised concerns that Brazil could be forced to return to the ranks of net oil importers for the first time since 2014.
Brazil is expected to continue holding near-annual Open Acreage concession and production sharing bid rounds after updating rules
-- Sheky Espejo, Jeff Fick

Platts to launch STS Lome Gasoline differentials to Eurobob FOB AR assessments
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will launch a new daily price assessment reflecting the differential between the Gasoline STS Lome assessment and the Gasoline Eurobob FOB AR barge assessment, effective Nov. 23, 2026.
This follows increased demand for transparency on West African gasoline differentials, as Nigeria's Dangote refinery continues to reshape flows and pricing dynamics in the Atlantic Basin.
The assessed differential is calculated as the difference

between the physical Gasoline STS Lome assessment and the midpoint of the three-to-10-day forward strip, itself derived from the established Gasoline Eurobob FOB AR barge assessment derivative assessments.
The new assessments will provide a transparent indication of the value of STS Lome gasoline relative to Northwest European gasoline blendstock assessments, and will complement Platts existing outright STS Lome gasoline differentials against Platts Premium 10ppm FOB Mediterranean cargoes and Premium 10ppm FOB Rotterdam barges.

Assessment Gasoline STS Lome Diff (EBOB) Gasoline STS Lome Diff (EBOB) MAvg

Description Gasoline STS Lome vs Gasoline Eurobob FOB AR barge Gasoline STS Lome vs Gasoline Eurobob FOB AR barge MAvg

Symbol To be announced
To be announced

Platts will assess the differential in $/metric ton, reflecting the difference between the relevant STS Lome gasoline assessment and the physical Platts Eurobob FOB AR assessment.
Platts will publish the new assessments in $/mt, timestamped at 4.30 pm London time, as well as a monthly average.
The new gasoline assessments will be found in European Marketscan and on Platts Global Alert and Platts Refined Products Alert fixed page 1122.
Platts continues to monitor evolving West Africa oil product flows and seeks industry feedback on its existing West African refined oil products assessment methodology.

Platts to amend freight calculation for Oman, Murban CFR North Asia crude assessments Nov. 2
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will amend the freight calculation used in its daily Oman and Murban CFR North Asia crude assessments, effective Nov. 2, 2026.
Under the change, Platts will use the daily VLCC Gulf of Omanto-Far East freight assessment in $/mt (DOOEW00), which was launched Aug. 3, in its calculation of the outright price of Oman and Murban delivered into North Asia at 4:30 pm Singapore time (0830 GMT).
The change will better reflect the shipping economics for the two grades, with Murban primarily loading from Fujairah port and Oman from Mina Al Fahal port outside the Strait of Hormuz.
Currently, the freight calculation is based on the daily VLCC Persian Gulf-to-Far East freight assessment in $/mt (TDAAB00).
Platts first proposed the change in a subscriber note published Sept. 28, 2026.
The freight calculation will continue to use the conversion factor for metric tons to barrels, as applied to the grades, in line with their prevailing qualities. The conversion factors are currently 7.32 for Oman and 7.62 for Murban.

In line with this change, Platts will also use the daily VLCC Gulf of Oman-to-Far East worldscale freight assessment (DIOEW00) in the freight calculation for Murban in its Indonesian Minas, Cinta and Widuri crude market parity prices.
Under the current MPP methodology for Minas, Cinta and Widuri, Murban -- as one of five basket grades -- is normalized for delivery to Singapore, accounting for freight. This is currently calculated using the daily VLCC Persian Gulf-to-Singapore worldscale freight assessment (PFAOD00).

Platts amends Abu Dhabi crude OSPs publication Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, has updated its publication
of Abu Dhabi crude official selling prices effective Oct. 8, 2026, in line with changes announced by Abu Dhabi National Oil Co. (ADNOC).
In July, ADNOC said it would adopt a prompt-month pricing methodology based on the Platts Dubai benchmark (PCAAT00) plus an ADNOC-announced differential for the OSPs of its Abu Dhabi onshore and offshore crude grades, with effect from November 2026 loadings. ADNOC added that it would announce the OSP differentials for Murban, Das, Umm Lulu and Upper Zakum in the month prior to the delivery month.
Accordingly, Platts will publish the monthly OSP differentials for the four grades, basis Platts Dubai, beginning with the November differentials issued by ADNOC on Oct. 8, 2026.
Platts first announced the change in a subscriber note published on Aug. 31, 2026.
The OSP differentials are published under the following reinstated codes:

Grade Murban Umm Lulu Das Blend Upper Zakum

OSP Diff to Dubai AAIOS00 OSLUD00 AAXPL00 AAIOV00

Platts previously published the OSP differentials under these codes until May 2021, when they were then suspended due to a change in the ADNOC pricing methodology.
From November, the existing codes for the outright OSPs for Murban (AAIKZ00), Upper Zakum (AAILC00), Das Blend (AAXPH00) and Umm Lulu (OSLUF00) will remain unchanged. Platts will publish the outright OSPs of the four grades following the publication of the average of Platts Dubai crude assessments during the month of loading.
In view of the changes, Platts is no longer updating the codes reflecting Upper Zakum, Das Blend and Umm Lulu OSP differentials against Murban, as well as the codes for all four grades, including Murban, calculated against the monthly average of Platts cash Dubai 1st Line assessment in the month of trade.
The following codes will no longer be updated from October:

Grade Murban Umm Lulu Das Blend Upper Zakum

Diff to Dubai AOSPA00 AOSPB00 AOSPC00 AOSPD00

Diff to Murban OSP
AOSPE00 AOSPF00 AOSPG00

ADNOC's OSPs are published in the Platts price database and on Platts Global Alert page 1051.

Platts to launch daily FOB Middle East bitumen assessments from Nov. 2
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will launch daily VG30 and VG40 bitumen spot assessments for FOB Middle East, effective Nov. 2, 2026.
The new assessments will complement Platts' existing daily and weekly bitumen assessments, providing greater price transparency and information in the waterborne trade for the product in the Asia-Pacific and Middle East markets.
Platts will launch two new daily outright assessments and

their respective monthly averages. These assessments will reflect bulk viscosity grade 30 and VG40 grade bitumen, as per IS 73:2013 specifications, loading 10-30 days forward from the date of publication in the Middle East.

Assessment FOB Middle East

Pricing information for material with other specifications, such as PEN 60/70, may be considered for the assessments, normalized to the proposed standard.
The assessments will reflect market value at the close of Asian trading at 4:30 pm Singapore time and follow the Singapore publication schedule. Bids, offers and trades reported throughout the day may be considered in the assessment process, with values normalized to the market close at 4:30 pm Singapore time.
This follows a proposal published Sept. 14 and available here. Please send any questions, comments or feedback to asia_products@spglobal.com, with a copy to pricegroup@spglobal.com. Platts revises measurement units, descriptions for FO Licht ethanol prices
The following Platts FOLicht ethanol prices have had their units of measurement and descriptions updated, as summarized below.
Cubic Meter Pure Alcohol (CPA) · Type: Volume Unit · Definition: A unit of volume that measures the volume of pure (100%) alcohol. One CBMPA equals 1 cubic meter (1,000 liters) of pure alcohol. Unlike a standard cubic meter (m³), which measures the total volume of any substance (liquid, gas, or solid), CBMPA refers specifically to the ethanol content, not the total liquid volume.

Platts proposes to launch weighted indexes for Chicago CBOB, ULSD
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, is proposing to launch Chicago basket indexes for gasoline and diesel from March 2, incorporating the independent assessments for the major pipelines and Buckeye Complex and weighting them by trading volume, as well as adding a new, separate assessment for

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

47

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 26, 2026

Chicago West Shore and Badger pipeline ULSD. Feedback from market participants has indicated the need
for Chicago-weighted basket indexes that reflect recent volumes traded on the Badger, West Shore, and Wolverine pipelines, as well as the Buckeye Complex.
The proposed weightings would be calculated by tracking recent trades across the West Shore, Badger, and Wolverine pipelines, as well as the Buckeye Complex. Total reported trades would be compiled on a cyclical basis, assuming a minimum volume of 10,000 barrels where not specified. These trades will then be sorted by pipeline to determine the percentage contribution of each component.
The weighted index would be calculated from the average of the differentials to NYMEX futures for the West Shore/Badger pipelines, Wolverine pipeline and BCX assessments, adjusted by their corresponding weightings.
For example, if Platts is assessing Cycle 2 for Chicago ULSD and traded volumes from the previous cycles came out to 20% West Shore and Badger, 30% Wolverine and 50% Buckeye Complex and the pipeline differentials were assessed at plus 2 cents/gal, plus 3 cents/gal and plus 3.50 cents/gal, respectively, the index basket differential would be plus 3.05 cents/gal.
Platts' current assessments for Chicago CBOB and ULSD are as follows:

Symbol AATHA00 AGCWC00 ACBAE00 AAREL00 ACBAA00 AGCWA00

Name ULSD Chicago pipeline ULSD Chicago Wolverine Pipeline ULSD Chicago BCX Gasoline CBOB Chicago pipeline Gasoline CBOB Chicago BCX Gasoline CBOB Chicago Wolverine Pipeline

The proposed Chicago basket indexes would be as follows:
Name Gasoline CBOB Chicago Basket ULSD Chicago Basket

Platts is concurrently seeking feedback on how to incorporate West Shore and Badger into the weighted index when the 1 psi waiver is in place during the summer. More information on how Platts will adjust the West Shore and Badger assessments in accordance with the 1 psi waiver can be found here.
Additionally, Platts seeks feedback on the naming conventions for the proposal. Platts proposes to amend the assessment name from Gasoline CBOB Chicago pipeline to Gasoline CBOB West Shore and Badger pipeline to better represent the products assessed. This change follows the Feb. 3, 2025, removal of the Wolverine Pipeline from the basket underpinning the generic Chicago assessment due to the anticipated removal of the 1 psi waiver in multiple Midwest states and a division in RVP.

Platts is also proposing to launch a separate West Shore/ Badger assessment for Chicago ULSD; this would not replace the existing generic assessment, which would remain unchanged. The name of the new assessment would be ULSD Chicago West Shore and Badger Pipeline.
The indexes would appear on PGA pages 350, 351, 154, 24, 482, 410 and 411, as well as in US Marketscan, Oilgram Price Report, and on Platts Connect.
Please send all feedback and comments by Jan. 23 to americas_products@spglobal.com and pricegroup@spglobal.com.
For written comments, please provide a clear indication if comments are not intended for publication by Platts for public viewing. Platts will consider all comments received and will make comments not marked as confidential available upon request.
Platts invites feedback on US gasoline RVP calendar for 2026 As part of its commitment to open and transparent pricing
and product specifications, Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, would like to invite feedback regarding publishing dates and/or cycles for 2026 US gasoline RVP shifts.
The 2026 calendar largely follows 2025 norms, with no notable deviations from prior years. The calendar will be updated throughout the year as more information becomes available.
The shift dates are based on relevant pipeline schedules, historical information, and feedback from market participants.
US Gulf Coast shifts are based on the Colonial Pipeline schedule. US Atlantic Coast shifts are based on Buckeye Pipeline and Colonial Pipeline calendars.
On the West Coast, Los Angeles, San Francisco and Arizona RVP changes are based on Kinder Morgan's SFPP schedule, while Portland and Seattle shifts are based on Olympic Pipeline's calendar.
In the Midwest, Group 3 shifts are based on the ONEOK Pipeline schedule, while Chicago shifts are based on Explorer Pipeline and Buckeye Complex schedules.
The US Environmental Protection Agency removed a waiver in eight Midwestern states that allowed for an additional 1 psi during the summer. Platts will adjust the RVP Calendar for Chicago CBOB in accordance with the implementation of the waiver. More information can be found here.
For questions or comments, please contact americas_products@spglobal.com with a cc to pricegroup@spglobal.com.
For written comments, please provide a clear indication if comments are not intended for publication by Platts for public viewing. Platts will consider all comments received and will make comments not marked as confidential available upon request.

3-DAY TRAINING

Oil Markets Fundamentals & Commercial Strategies

Nov 3-5, 2026 | Singapore

Dec 8-10, 2026 | Dubai

Mar 23-25, 2027 | Singapore Apr 6-8, 2027 | Houston

Apr 27-29, 2026 | London

May 11-12, 2027 | Abuja

Jun 22-24, 2027 | Istanbul

Jun 30-Jul 1 | Kuala Lumpur

Oil markets are increasingly complex, shaped by shifting supply and demand, geopolitical developments, changing trade flows and price volatility. This course provides a practical understanding of the forces shaping global crude oil and refined product markets.
Explore market fundamentals, price formation and forecasting, trading, and risk management, and learn how to translate market developments into better commercial decisions.
Build the market knowledge and commercial insight to make more confident decisions in a rapidly evolving oil market.
WHO SHOULD ATTEND The course would be suitable for:
· Technical and non-technical professionals working across crude oil and refined products
· Traders, brokers and market analysts · Professionals in commercial, planning, business development,
supply and distribution roles · Shipping and logistics professionals · New industry entrants seeking a practical understanding of oil
markets

Instructors
Kaushik Burman Roy Global Director, Energy Training, S&P Global
Ha Nguyen Head of Oil & Fuels, Long-Term Research, S&P Global
Daniel Colover Head of Oil, Chemicals and EMEA Markets Engagement Program, S&P Global

2-DAY TRAINING
Upstream Fundamentals and Economics
June 2-3 | Houston

Fossil fuels dominate the energy mix representing over 80% of global energy demand. With geopolitical tensions influencing supply volumes and lower price, the S&P Global Commodity Insights "Upstream Fundamentals and Economics" course will equip you with the insights and strategies needed to navigate a constantly changing environment, meet challenges with informed decisions, and drive business growth.
Our prestigious Upstream Fundamentals and Economics Training is a great opportunity to hear from a range of S&P Global experts sharing their expertise, regional trends and industry insights, as well as concrete case studies and analyses.
WHO SHOULD ATTEND
This course would be suitable for:
· Technical and non-technical professionals, with experience or new recruits, from crude oil and refined products companies
· Governments, EPCs, OEMs, Suppliers, Investors · Personnel from Projects, Planning, Business Development,
Commercial
